 241: prime-supported Euler products and the elementary \(2\pi i\)-periodicity
 242: of scalar determinant models after \(z=e^{-s}\); these statements are
 243: not used in the finite-budget proofs.
 244: 
 245: \subsection{Relation to prior work}\label{subsec:prior-work}
 246: 
 247: We give a theorem-by-theorem account of what is known, what is a
 248: specialized reproof, and what is new.
 249: 
 250: \begin{itemize}
 251:   \item \textbf{\suppref{Theorem~1.2} (density dichotomy):
 252:   classical.} The eventual periodicity of normalized word counts for a
 253:   deterministic automaton is a direct Perron--Frobenius consequence and
 254:   is standard in the density literature for regular languages; see
 255:   \cite{Berstel1973,SalomaaSoittola1978,BodirskyGaertnerVonOertzenSchwinghammer2004,Bell2020UpperDensity,Koga2019Density,BerstelReutenauer2011}.
 256:   We record it only in the residue-class form needed for the later
 257:   prime estimates.
 258: 
 259:   \item \textbf{\suppref{Lemma~1.16} and
 260:   \Cref{prop:zeckendorf-regular-powerlaw}: classical.} These results are standard residue-class regroupings of
 261:   the periodic Perron--Frobenius theorem for trim strongly connected
 262:   presentations and of the known Zeckendorf growth law; see, e.g.,
 263:   \cite{SalomaaSoittola1978,LecomteRigo2001,CharlierRampersad2011}.
 264: 
 265:   \item \textbf{Multicomponent theorem.}
 266:   \suppref{Theorem~1.14} keeps the
 267:   residue-wise exponential-polynomial expansion on the Perron-scale
 268:   active classes of arbitrary trim deterministic presentations, absorbs
 269:   lower-growth classes into the sub-Perron term, and compares the
 270:   polynomial exponents in the product automaton. This is the
 271:   presentation-level finite-budget result used to handle maximal
 272:   components connected by transient paths.
 273: 
 274:   \item \textbf{\suppref{Theorem~1.18} (trim strongly connected
 275:   presentations).} This is the \(s_r=0\) specialization of the
 276:   multicomponent picture and agrees with the conditional-density
 277:   literature
 278:   \cite{HanselPerrin1989,Kozik2005Conditional,Koga2019Density,Montoya2025Relative,BertheGouletPerrin2025}.
 279:   Its role is to isolate the constants used in the Zeckendorf
 280:   application.
 281: 
 282:   \item \textbf{Effective quantitative prime-slice obstructions.} The
 283:   introductory binary corollaries above summarize the later statements
 284:   \suppref{Proposition~1.9}, \suppref{Corollary~1.10}, and
 285:   \suppref{Corollary~1.12}.
 286:   The effective theorem \suppref{Theorem~1.7}
 287:   makes the fixed-base constants and thresholds computable from a
 288:   compressed Markov-chain certificate for the transition matrix of the
 289:   given DFA. This is an effective packaging of the classical density
 290:   dichotomy with explicit prime-counting input, not a new density
 291:   dichotomy. Together with
 292:   \suppref{Corollary~1.19}, this gives a uniform
 293:   obstruction to approximating the base-\(b\) prime slices by a single
 294:   fixed DFA, both for symmetric difference and for simultaneous
 295:   recall/precision. The Zeckendorf analogues
 296:   \Cref{cor:zeck-symmetric-diff,cor:zeck-recall-precision} follow by the
 297:   same mechanism.
 298: 
 299:   \item \textbf{Coprime-quotient Zeckendorf pumping
 300:   (\Cref{thm:coprime-quotient-zeckendorf-pumping}).} The PNT-free
 301:   unimodular block-action argument is the primary new Zeckendorf
 302:   language theorem in this note. It uses
 303:   \Cref{lem:zeckendorf-unimodular-block-action,lem:zeckendorf-pumping-congruence}
 304:   with modulus \(N_i^2\) to build an infinite chain
 305:   \(N_{i+1}=N_iQ_i\) with \(Q_i\equiv1\pmod {N_i}\) inside any
 306:   infinite regular \(L\subseteq\mathcal Z\). This implies that new
 307:   rational prime divisors occur along the chain, hence
 308:   \[
 309:     \sup_{w\in L}\omega(\val(w))=\infty.
 310:   \]
 311:   The bounded-distinct-prime and finite-prime-support REG-immunity
 312:   corollaries are the main language-theoretic consequences; bounded
 313:   almost-prime and prime-language REG-immunity are weaker downstream
 314:   cases. The slice-rigidity statement
 315:   \(\Cref{prop:zeckendorf-regular-slice-rigidity}\) remains a separate
 316:   density-scale obstruction, while the nonregularity corollary
 317:   \(\Cref{cor:zeckendorf-prime-language-not-regular}\) follows
 318:   immediately from REG-immunity. The sofic-slice exclusion
 319:   \(\Cref{rem:zeckendorf-primes-not-sofic}\) is then a short consequence
 320:   of regularity of sofic block languages.
 321: 
 322:   \item \textbf{Context-free, MCFL, and Pisot extensions.}
 323:   Fixed-base prime CF-immunity is classical. Hartmanis and Shank proved
 324:   that no infinite set of primes can be accepted in fixed-base notation
 325:   by a pushdown automaton; Sch\"utzenberger obtained the conclusion
 326:   independently \cite{HartmanisShank1968,Schutzenberger1968}. We do not
 327:   reproduce their proofs as new. Our use of Ogden's lemma
 328:   \cite{Ogden1968} instead couples the two pumped blocks to the
 329:   Zeckendorf unimodular action and yields the stronger coprime-quotient
 330:   chain
 331:   \Cref{thm:context-free-coprime-quotient-zeckendorf-pumping}. The
 332:   weak pumping lemma of Seki--Matsumura--Fujii--Kasami
 333:   \cite{SekiEtAl1991} supplies one synchronized \(2k\)-block orbit for
 334:   every infinite \(k\)-MCFL; it is used as an input, not reproved.
 335:   Kanazawa--Kobele--Michaelis--Salvati--Yoshinaka prove that the usual
 336:   strong pumping form fails in general
 337:   \cite{KanazawaKobeleMichaelisSalvatiYoshinaka2014}, and the recent
 338:   substitution lemma of Duncan--Elder--Frenkel--Liu gives a different
 339:   strong interface \cite{DuncanElderFrenkelLiu2026}. Neither result
 340:   concerns recurrence numeration or divisibility. The new deductions
 341:   are the fixed local-congruence orbit
 342:   \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}, the topological
 343:   fingerprint \Cref{thm:mcfl-local-adic-fingerprint}, the exact rank
 344:   formula \Cref{prop:local-prime-factor-cb-rank}, and the escape
 345:   dichotomy \Cref{thm:nonunit-mcfl-escape-dichotomy}. The unit-system
 346:   constructions are \Cref{thm:unit-mcfl-deep-congruence-chain} and
 347:   \Cref{thm:unit-mcfl-divisibility-tree}.
 348:   Evertse's theorem on prime ideals in ratios of terms of
 349:   nondegenerate recurrences \cite{Evertse1984} is used as an arithmetic
 350:   input to the geometric-ray characterization
 351:   \Cref{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray}; it is not claimed as
 352:   a new recurrence theorem. The local return congruence further confines the
 353:   geometric ratio to primes dividing the minimal tail constant, and greedy
 354:   length thresholds give the integer/nonintegral Pisot classification in
 355:   \Cref{lem:geometric-ratio-tail-support,cor:linear-pisot-bounded-support-classification}.
 356:   Broughan's \(\mathbb Q\)- and Cantor-space classifications for adic
 357:   integer topologies are cited as classical inputs
 358:   \cite{Broughan2003}. Frougny normalization and the
 359:   Bertrand and substitution literature
 360:   \cite{Frougny1992,BruyereHansel1997,Fabre1995,FrougnySolomyak1992}
 361:   supply the canonical finite-state numeration interface, not the
 362:   arithmetic pumping proof. The residual nonunit and non-positional
 363:   limits are stated in \Cref{rem:pisot-open-interfaces}. The
 364:   recurrence-witness separation
 365:   \Cref{prop:recurrence-witness-separation} distinguishes the invariant
 366:   minimal action from an arbitrarily inflated companion presentation.
 367: 
 368:   \item \textbf{Analytic obstructions: classical background.}
 369:   \suppref{Theorem~A.1} is the usual
 370:   P\'olya--Carlson natural-boundary mechanism for Euler products.
 371:   \suppref{Proposition~A.2} is the corresponding
 372:   periodicity obstruction for determinant models after \(z=e^{-s}\). Both are
 373:   classical
 374:   \cite{Polya1923,Carlson1921,Estermann1928,ArtinMazur1965,BowenLanford1970}.
 375: \end{itemize}
 376: \smallskip
 377: \noindent\textbf{Relation to density and approximation literature.} For ordinary
 378: automatic sets, asymptotic density questions go back at least to
 379: Berstel \cite{Berstel1973}; the standard global sources include
 380: B\"uchi's automata-theoretic decidability work \cite{Buchi1960} and
 381: Allouche--Shallit's monograph on automatic sequences

2-
3-                     Adic Topologies
4-                     for the Rational Integers
5-
6-                                Kevin A. Broughan
7-
8-                                                 Abstract. A topology on Z, which gives a nice proof that the set of prime integers is infinite, is char-
9-                                                 acterised and examined. It is found to be homeomorphic to Q, with a compact completion homeo-
10:                                                 morphic to the Cantor set. It has a natural place in a family of topologies on Z, which includes the
11:                                                 p-adics, and one in which the set of rational primes P is dense. Examples from number theory are
12-                                                 given, including the primes and squares, Fermat numbers, Fibonacci numbers and k-free numbers.
13-
14-                         1 Introduction
15-
16-                                       There is a nice topology on Z, highlighted in reference [2], which enables a very
17:                                       elegant proof to be given that the number of rational primes is infinite. In this paper
18-                                       we develop properties of this topology, define a class of metrics which generate it,
19-                                       establish a natural family of topologies (the adic topologies) of which this is the finest
20-                                       and which includes the p-adic topologies, and give some examples from number
21-                                       theory.
22-
23-                                          The motivation behind this work is to provide some tools which will assist with
24-                                       the description and comparison of sets of integers, which are of number theoretic
25-                                       interest.

4-                                                           ANDREW DUNCAN, MURRAY ELDER, LISA FRENKEL, AND MENGFAN LYU
5-
6-                                                     Abstract. We present a new criterion for proving that a language is not multiple context-
7-                                                     free, which we call a Substitution Lemma. We apply it to show a sample selection of languages
8-                                                     are not multiple context-free, including the word problem of F2 × F2.
9-
10-                                                          Our result is in contrast to Kanazawa et al. [2014, Theory Comput. Syst.] who proved
11:                                                     that it was not possible to generalise the standard pumping lemma for context-free languages
12-                                                     to multiple context-free languages, and Kanazawa [2019, Inform. and Comput.] who showed a
13-                                                     weak variant of generalised Ogden's lemma does not apply to multiple context-free languages.
14-
15-                                                          We also record that groups with multiple context-free word problem have decidable rational
16-                                                     subset membership problem.
17-
18-                                                                                           1. Introduction
19-
20-                                        Multiple context-free languages were introduced by Seki, Matsumura, Fujii and Kasami [24, 35].
21-                                        They were proposed as an attempt to describe the syntax of natural languages (see for example
22-                                        [6]), but were later shown not to suffice when Salvati proved that the language MIX31 is multiple
23-                                        context-free [34], since MIX3 is regarded as a language that should not appear in any class
24-                                        capturing the syntax of natural languages [17].
25-
26-                                        A useful tool for working with formal language classes is a criterion to show that a certain
27:                                        language is not in the class. For example, the well-known pumping lemmas for regular and
28-                                        context-free (CF) languages (see for example [36]), Ogden's lemma (see Lemma 5.5) and the
29-                                        Interchange lemma for CF languages [32, 33], Gilman's Shrinking lemma for indexed languages
30-                                        [13], Mitrana and Stiebe's Interchange lemma and the Swapping lemma of the second author for
31-                                        blind multi-counter languages [29, 10].
32-
33:                                        Seki et al. [35] gave what was later called a weak pumping lemma for multiple context-free
34:                                        languages, which is weak in the sense that pumping only applies to a single word in the language,
35:                                        rather than all words that are sufficiently long (see Lemma 5.3). Kanazawa et al. [22] proved
36:                                        that it was not possible to generalise the standard pumping lemma for context-free languages
37-                                        to multiple context-free languages, and Kanazawa also showed a weak variant of generalised
38-                                        Ogden's lemma fails [21]. In this article we present a criterion which we call a Substitution
39-                                        Lemma (Theorem 4.5) for an infinite language to be multiple context-free, as an alternative type
40:                                        of pumping lemma for multiple context-free languages, which is strong in the sense that the
41-                                        substitution applies to all words in the language that are of sufficient length. Our result can
42-
43-                                            Date: September 29, 2025.
44-                                            2020 Mathematics Subject Classification. 20F10, 68Q45.
45-                                            Key words and phrases. multiple context-free language; k-restricted tree stack automaton; substitution lemma.
46-                                            1MIXn = {w  {a1, . . . , an} | |w|a1 = · · · = |w|an } where |w|x is the number of occurrences of the letter x
47-                                        in w.
48-
--
50-                                                                                                                                                                                   2
51-
52-be viewed as a generalisation of Ogden's and the Interchange lemmas [32, 33]. Its proof relies
53-on a characterisation of k-multiple context-free languages as those accepted by k-restricted tree
54-stack automata due to Denkinger [7]. As a demonstration, in Section 5 we use the Substitution
55-Lemma to show that some languages are not k-multiple context-free for some or all k, including
56-languages whose Parikh image is semi-linear.
57-
58:A key motivation for devising a new type of pumping lemma is to understand groups which have
59-multiple context-free word problem, see subsection 5.1. In Section 6 we observe that the rational
60-subset membership problem is decidable for groups with multiple context-free word problem.
61-Lastly in Appendix A we include a self-contained proof that the languages accepted by a 1-
62-restricted tree stack automaton are exactly the context-free languages. Other recent work on
63-properties and applications of multiple context-free languages includes [9, 27].

import unittest

from artifacts import verify_pisot_pumping as verifier


class PisotPumpingVerifierTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_linear_pisot_decision_accepts_exactly_integer_bases(self):
        decisions = {
            system.name: verifier.has_bounded_outside_support_mcfl(system)
            for system in verifier.SYSTEMS
        }

        self.assertEqual(
            decisions,
            {
                "fibonacci": False,
                "pell": False,
                "tribonacci": False,
                "quadratic_nonunit": False,
                "integer_base_2": True,
            },
        )

    def test_geometric_ratio_uses_only_tail_primes(self):
        self.assertIsNone(
            verifier.geometric_ratio_support_obstruction(
                trailing_coefficient=2, initial_factor=5, ratio=8
            )
        )
        obstruction = verifier.geometric_ratio_support_obstruction(
            trailing_coefficient=2, initial_factor=5, ratio=6
        )
        self.assertEqual(
            obstruction,
            {
                "prime": 3,
                "modulus": 3,
                "initial_valuation": 0,
            },
        )
        self.assertEqual(
            verifier.geometric_ratio_support_obstruction(
                trailing_coefficient=1, initial_factor=12, ratio=2
            ),
            {"prime": 2, "modulus": 8, "initial_valuation": 2},
        )

    def test_tail_action_starts_after_the_eventual_recurrence_transient(self):
        recurrence = (-10, 7)
        weights = (1, 3, 6, 12, 24, 48, 96, 192)
        prefix = (2,)

        for suffix_length in range(6):
            for suffix_number in range(1 << suffix_length):
                suffix = tuple(
                    (suffix_number >> index) & 1
                    for index in range(suffix_length)
                )
                transformed = verifier.tail_action_state(
                    recurrence, weights, prefix, suffix
                )
                full_word = prefix + suffix
                expected = (
                    sum(
                        digit * weight
                        for digit, weight in zip(full_word, weights)
                    ),
                    *weights[len(full_word) : len(full_word) + len(recurrence)],
                )
                self.assertEqual(transformed, expected)

    def test_explicit_linear_mcfg_ray_realizes_a_geometric_base_two_orbit(self):
        base_two = verifier.system_by_name("integer_base_2")
        values = []
        for exponent in range(13):
            word = verifier.linear_mcfg_ray_word(
                prefix=(),
                constants=((), (1,)),
                left_pumps=((0,),),
                middles=((),),
                right_pumps=((),),
                exponent=exponent,
            )
            self.assertEqual(word, (0,) * exponent + (1,))
            values.append(verifier.value(base_two, word))

        self.assertEqual(values, [2**exponent for exponent in range(13)])

    def test_affine_action_matches_direct_evaluation(self):
        for system in verifier.SYSTEMS:
            with self.subTest(system=system.name):
                verifier.check_affine_action(system, max_length=7)

    def test_two_block_return_gives_square_modulus_congruence(self):
        fib = verifier.system_by_name("fibonacci")
        word = (0, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1)  # 23 in LSD-first Zeckendorf form
        witness = verifier.verify_pump_witness(
            fib,
            word,
            cuts=(2, 3, 4, 5),
            require_canonical=True,
        )
        self.assertEqual(witness.original_value, 23)
        self.assertGreater(witness.pump_exponent, 1)
        self.assertEqual(witness.pumped_value % (23 * 23), 23)
        self.assertGreater(witness.pumped_value, 23)
        self.assertEqual(witness.pumped_value // 23 % 23, 1)

    def test_nonunit_bad_prime_is_rejected(self):
        nonunit = verifier.system_by_name("quadratic_nonunit")
        with self.assertRaises(verifier.NonInvertibleAction):
            verifier.matrix_order(
                verifier.block_matrix(nonunit, (0,)), modulus=2
            )

    def test_counterexample_search_finds_required_hypothesis(self):
        witness = verifier.search_singular_counterexample()
        self.assertEqual(witness["system"], "quadratic_nonunit")
        self.assertEqual(witness["modulus"], 2)
        self.assertEqual(witness["trailing_coefficient"], 2)
        self.assertFalse(witness["invertible"])

    def test_full_verification_suite_has_no_congruence_failures(self):
        report = verifier.run_verification()
        self.assertGreaterEqual(report["systems_checked"], 5)
        self.assertGreater(report["affine_cases"], 100)
        self.assertGreater(report["pump_witnesses"], 0)
        self.assertEqual(report["congruence_failures"], 0)
        self.assertEqual(report["counterexample"]["modulus"], 2)
        self.assertGreater(report["synchronized_orbit_cases"], 100)
        self.assertEqual(report["local_layer_isolation_failures"], 0)
        self.assertEqual(report["deep_chain_failures"], 0)
        self.assertEqual(report["divisibility_tree_failures"], 0)
        self.assertGreater(report["inflated_fibonacci_cases"], 100)
        self.assertEqual(report["inflated_fibonacci_failures"], 0)
        self.assertGreater(report["tail_prefix_cases"], 50)
        self.assertEqual(report["tail_prefix_failures"], 0)
        self.assertEqual(report["geometric_ray_cases"], 13)
        self.assertEqual(report["geometric_ray_failures"], 0)
        self.assertEqual(report["linear_pisot_classification_cases"], 5)
        self.assertEqual(report["linear_pisot_classification_failures"], 0)
        self.assertEqual(report["geometric_ratio_support_cases"], 4)
        self.assertEqual(report["geometric_ratio_support_failures"], 0)

    def test_inflated_fibonacci_has_unit_reachable_action(self):
        report = verifier.verify_inflated_fibonacci_separation(
            primes=(2, 3, 5, 7, 11, 13),
            maximum_power=4,
            maximum_index=24,
        )
        self.assertGreater(report["cases"], 100)
        self.assertEqual(report["failures"], 0)
        self.assertEqual(report["reachable_rank"], 2)
        self.assertEqual(report["reachable_determinant"], -1)

    def test_synchronized_multi_block_orbit_for_all_tested_parameters(self):
        fibonacci = verifier.system_by_name("fibonacci")
        word = (0, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1)
        spans = ((2, 3), (4, 5))
        cases = verifier.check_synchronized_orbit(
            fibonacci,
            word,
            spans,
            moduli=range(2, 21),
            parameters=range(6),
        )
        self.assertEqual(cases, 19 * 6)

        nonunit = verifier.system_by_name("quadratic_nonunit")
        with self.assertRaises(verifier.NonInvertibleAction):
            verifier.check_synchronized_orbit(
                nonunit,
                (1, 0, 0, 1),
                ((1, 2), (2, 3)),
                moduli=(2,),
                parameters=(0,),
            )

    def test_local_layer_isolation_certificate(self):
        n = 2 * 3 * 5**2 * 7
        excluded_primes = (2, 3)
        valuation_bounds = {2: 2, 3: 1}
        modulus = verifier.local_layer_isolation_modulus(
            n, excluded_primes, valuation_bounds
        )
        self.assertEqual(verifier.omega_outside(n, excluded_primes), 2)
        self.assertEqual(verifier.gcd(modulus, 2 * 3), 1)

        congruent_layer_points = [
            m
            for m in range(1, 50_001)
            if verifier.in_local_prime_layer(
                m, 2, excluded_primes, valuation_bounds
            )
            and (m - n) % modulus == 0
        ]
        self.assertEqual(congruent_layer_points, [n])

    def test_deep_chain_has_prescribed_congruence_depth(self):
        specifications = ((6, 1), (30, 2), (210, 1))
        chain = verifier.construct_deep_congruence_chain(2, specifications)
        for current, following, (modulus_factor, depth) in zip(
            chain, chain[1:], specifications
        ):
            quotient = following // current
            self.assertGreater(quotient, 1)
            self.assertEqual(following % current, 0)
            self.assertEqual(
                quotient % (modulus_factor * current**depth), 1
            )

    def test_finite_divisibility_tree_is_induced_and_has_coprime_edges(self):
        nodes = ((), (0,), (1,), (0, 0), (0, 1), (1, 0))
        thresholds = {
            (0,): 3,
            (1,): 5,
            (0, 0): 7,
            (0, 1): 11,
            (1, 0): 13,
        }
        values, edge_quotients = verifier.construct_divisibility_tree(
            root=2, nodes=nodes, thresholds=thresholds
        )
        for left in nodes:
            for right in nodes:
                self.assertEqual(
                    values[right] % values[left] == 0,
                    right[: len(left)] == left,
                )
        quotients = list(edge_quotients.values())
        for index, left in enumerate(quotients):
            for right in quotients[index + 1 :]:
                self.assertEqual(verifier.gcd(left, right), 1)
        for edge, quotient in edge_quotients.items():
            for prime in range(2, thresholds[edge] + 1):
                if verifier.is_prime(prime):
                    self.assertNotEqual(quotient % prime, 0)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

 320:                 pump_disjoint_blocks(word, spans, parameter + return_time),
 321:             )
 322:             if returned % modulus != current % modulus:
 323:                 raise AssertionError(
 324:                     f"synchronized congruence failed for {system.name}, "
 325:                     f"q={modulus}, t={parameter}"
 326:                 )
 327:             tested += 1
 328:     return tested
 329: 
 330: 
 331: def is_prime(number: int) -> bool:
 332:     if number < 2:
 333:         return False
 334:     if number % 2 == 0:
 335:         return number == 2
 336:     divisor = 3
 337:     while divisor * divisor <= number:
 338:         if number % divisor == 0:
 339:             return False
 340:         divisor += 2
 341:     return True
 342: 
 343: 
 344: def prime_factors(number: int) -> tuple[int, ...]:
 345:     if number < 1:
 346:         raise ValueError("prime factorization is defined here only for positive integers")
 347:     factors = []
 348:     candidate = 2
 349:     remaining = number
 350:     while candidate * candidate <= remaining:
 351:         if remaining % candidate == 0:
 352:             factors.append(candidate)
 353:             while remaining % candidate == 0:
 354:                 remaining //= candidate
 355:         candidate = 3 if candidate == 2 else candidate + 2
 356:     if remaining > 1:
 357:         factors.append(remaining)
 358:     return tuple(factors)
 359: 
 360: 
 361: def valuation(number: int, prime: int) -> int:
 362:     exponent = 0
 363:     while number % prime == 0:
 364:         number //= prime
 365:         exponent += 1
 366:     return exponent
 367: 
 368: 
 369: def geometric_ratio_support_obstruction(
 370:     trailing_coefficient: int, initial_factor: int, ratio: int
 371: ) -> dict[str, int] | None:
 372:     """Return the first local-congruence obstruction to ``c * b**t``."""
 373:     if trailing_coefficient == 0 or initial_factor < 1 or ratio < 2:
 374:         raise ValueError("the tail, initial factor, and ratio must define a scheme")
 375:     for prime in prime_factors(ratio):
 376:         if trailing_coefficient % prime != 0:
 377:             exponent = valuation(initial_factor, prime)
 378:             return {
 379:                 "prime": prime,
 380:                 "modulus": prime ** (exponent + 1),
 381:                 "initial_valuation": exponent,
 382:             }
 383:     return None
 384: 
 385: 
 386: def has_bounded_outside_support_mcfl(system: LinearSystem) -> bool:
 387:     """Decision rule for systems whose recurrence polynomial is minimal Pisot."""
 388:     return system.dimension == 1
 389: 
 390: 
 391: def omega_outside(number: int, excluded_primes: Iterable[int]) -> int:
 392:     excluded = set(excluded_primes)
 393:     return sum(prime not in excluded for prime in prime_factors(number))
 394: 
 395: 
 396: def in_local_prime_layer(
 397:     number: int,
 398:     maximum_outside_primes: int,
 399:     excluded_primes: Iterable[int],
 400:     valuation_bounds: dict[int, int],
 401: ) -> bool:
 402:     excluded = tuple(excluded_primes)
 403:     return (
 404:         number >= 1
 405:         and omega_outside(number, excluded) <= maximum_outside_primes
 406:         and all(valuation(number, prime) <= valuation_bounds[prime] for prime in excluded)
 407:     )
 408: 
 409: 
 410: def local_layer_isolation_modulus(
 411:     number: int,
 412:     excluded_primes: Iterable[int],
 413:     valuation_bounds: dict[int, int],
 414: ) -> int:
 415:     excluded = tuple(excluded_primes)
 416:     if not in_local_prime_layer(
 417:         number, omega_outside(number, excluded), excluded, valuation_bounds
 418:     ):
 419:         raise ValueError("the point violates an excluded-prime valuation bound")
 420: 
 421:     outside = tuple(
 422:         prime for prime in prime_factors(number) if prime not in set(excluded)
 423:     )
 424:     outside_core = product_power = 1
 425:     prime_power_modulus = 1
 426:     for prime in outside:
 427:         exponent = valuation(number, prime)
 428:         product_power *= prime**exponent
 429:         prime_power_modulus *= prime ** (exponent + 1)
 430:     outside_core = product_power
 431: 
 432:     smooth_factors = [1]
 433:     for prime in excluded:
 434:         smooth_factors = [
 435:             factor * prime**exponent
 436:             for factor in smooth_factors
 437:             for exponent in range(valuation_bounds[prime] + 1)
 438:         ]
 439:     candidates = tuple(outside_core * factor for factor in smooth_factors)
 440:     differences = tuple(abs(candidate - number) for candidate in candidates if candidate != number)
 441: 
 442:     auxiliary = 2
 443:     forbidden = set(excluded) | set(outside)
 444:     while (
 445:         not is_prime(auxiliary)
 446:         or auxiliary in forbidden
 447:         or any(difference % auxiliary == 0 for difference in differences)
 448:     ):
 449:         auxiliary += 1
 450:     return prime_power_modulus * auxiliary
 451: 
 452: 
 453: def construct_deep_congruence_chain(
 454:     initial: int, specifications: Sequence[tuple[int, int]]
 455: ) -> tuple[int, ...]:
 456:     if initial < 1:
 457:         raise ValueError("the initial value must be positive")
 458:     chain = [initial]
 459:     for modulus_factor, depth in specifications:
 460:         if modulus_factor < 1 or depth < 1:
 461:             raise ValueError("modulus factors and depths must be positive")
 462:         current = chain[-1]
 463:         quotient = 1 + modulus_factor * current**depth
 464:         chain.append(current * quotient)
 465:     return tuple(chain)
 466: 
 467: 
 468: def construct_divisibility_tree(
 469:     root: int,
 470:     nodes: Sequence[tuple[int, ...]],
 471:     thresholds: dict[tuple[int, ...], int],
 472: ) -> tuple[dict[tuple[int, ...], int], dict[tuple[int, ...], int]]:
 473:     if not nodes or nodes[0] != () or root < 1:
 474:         raise ValueError("nodes must begin with the root and the root value must be positive")
 475:     values = {(): root}
 476:     edge_quotients: dict[tuple[int, ...], int] = {}
 477:     for node in nodes[1:]:
 478:         parent = node[:-1]
 479:         if parent not in values:
 480:             raise ValueError("every parent must precede its children")
 481:         threshold = thresholds[node]
 482:         small_prime_product = 1
 483:         for candidate in range(2, threshold + 1):
 484:             if is_prime(candidate):
 485:                 small_prime_product *= candidate
 486:         previous_product = root * small_prime_product
 487:         for quotient in edge_quotients.values():
 488:             previous_product *= quotient
 489:         parent_value = values[parent]
 490:         quotient = 1 + parent_value * previous_product
 491:         values[node] = parent_value * quotient
 492:         edge_quotients[node] = quotient
 493:     return values, edge_quotients
 494:

 496: def verify_pump_witness(
 497:     system: LinearSystem,
 498:     word: Word,
 499:     cuts: tuple[int, int, int, int],
 500:     require_canonical: bool = False,
 501: ) -> PumpWitness:
 502:     original = value(system, word)
 503:     if not is_prime(original):
 504:         raise ValueError("the original word must represent a prime")
 505:     if original and system.trailing_coefficient % original == 0:
 506:         raise NonInvertibleAction("the represented prime divides the trailing coefficient")
 507:     a, b, c, d = cuts
 508:     v, y = word[a:b], word[c:d]
 509:     orders = [
 510:         matrix_order(block_matrix(system, block), original * original)
 511:         for block in (v, y)
 512:         if block
 513:     ]
 514:     return_time = lcm(*orders)
 515:     exponent = 1 + return_time
 516:     result_word = pumped_word(word, cuts, exponent)
 517:     result_value = value(system, result_word)
 518:     before = is_canonical(system, word)
 519:     after = is_canonical(system, result_word)
 520:     if require_canonical and not (before and after):
 521:         raise AssertionError("the selected pumping does not preserve canonical syntax")
 522:     if result_value % (original * original) != original:
 523:         raise AssertionError("square-modulus pumping congruence failed")
 524:     return PumpWitness(
 525:         system=system.name,
 526:         original_word="".join(map(str, word)),
 527:         original_value=original,
 528:         cuts=cuts,
 529:         return_time=return_time,
 530:         pump_exponent=exponent,
 531:         pumped_length=len(result_word),
 532:         pumped_value=result_value,
 533:         quotient_mod_original=(result_value // original) % original,
 534:         canonical_before=before,
 535:         canonical_after=after,
 536:     )
 537: 
 538: 
 539: def check_affine_action(system: LinearSystem, max_length: int = 5) -> int:
 540:     cases = 0
 541:     initial_state = (0,) + system.initials
 542:     for length in range(max_length + 1):
 543:         for word in product(range(system.max_digit + 1), repeat=length):
 544:             transformed = matvec(block_matrix(system, word), initial_state)
 545:             expected_weights = weights(system, length + system.dimension)[length:]
 546:             expected = (value(system, word),) + tuple(expected_weights)
 547:             if transformed != expected:
 548:                 raise AssertionError(
 549:                     f"affine action mismatch for {system.name}, word={word}: "
 550:                     f"{transformed} != {expected}"
 551:                 )
 552:             cases += 1
 553:     return cases

 571: def verify_inflated_fibonacci_separation(
 572:     primes: Sequence[int] = (2, 3, 5, 7, 11, 13),
 573:     maximum_power: int = 4,
 574:     maximum_index: int = 24,
 575: ) -> dict[str, int]:
 576:     """Check the ambient/reachable determinant separation for Fibonacci tails."""
 577:     fibonacci = [1, 2]
 578:     while len(fibonacci) <= maximum_index + 3:
 579:         fibonacci.append(fibonacci[-1] + fibonacci[-2])
 580: 
 581:     reachable_state = (1, 2, 3)
 582:     reachable_next = (2, 3, 5)
 583:     reachable_action = ((0, 1), (1, 1))
 584:     reachable_determinant = determinant(reachable_action)
 585:     saturation_minors = (
 586:         reachable_state[0] * reachable_next[1]
 587:         - reachable_state[1] * reachable_next[0],
 588:         reachable_state[0] * reachable_next[2]
 589:         - reachable_state[2] * reachable_next[0],
 590:         reachable_state[1] * reachable_next[2]
 591:         - reachable_state[2] * reachable_next[1],
 592:     )
 593: 
 594:     cases = 0
 595:     failures = 0
 596: 
 597:     def check(condition: bool) -> None:
 598:         nonlocal cases, failures
 599:         cases += 1
 600:         failures += int(not condition)

  72: def identity(size: int) -> Matrix:
  73:     return tuple(
  74:         tuple(1 if row == column else 0 for column in range(size))
  75:         for row in range(size)
  76:     )
  77: 
  78: 
  79: def matmul(left: Matrix, right: Matrix, modulus: int | None = None) -> Matrix:
  80:     rows = len(left)
  81:     inner = len(right)
  82:     columns = len(right[0])
  83:     entries = []
  84:     for row in range(rows):
  85:         out_row = []
  86:         for column in range(columns):
  87:             value = sum(left[row][k] * right[k][column] for k in range(inner))
  88:             out_row.append(value if modulus is None else value % modulus)
  89:         entries.append(tuple(out_row))
  90:     return tuple(entries)
  91: 
  92: 
  93: def matvec(matrix: Matrix, vector: Sequence[int]) -> tuple[int, ...]:
  94:     return tuple(
  95:         sum(matrix[row][column] * vector[column] for column in range(len(vector)))
  96:         for row in range(len(matrix))
  97:     )
  98: 
  99: 
 100: def determinant(matrix: Matrix) -> int:
 101:     size = len(matrix)
 102:     if size == 1:
 103:         return matrix[0][0]
 104:     total = 0
 105:     for column, entry in enumerate(matrix[0]):
 106:         minor = tuple(
 107:             tuple(row[j] for j in range(size) if j != column)
 108:             for row in matrix[1:]
 109:         )
 110:         total += (-1) ** column * entry * determinant(minor)
 111:     return total
 112: 
 113: 
 114: def digit_matrix(system: LinearSystem, digit: int) -> Matrix:
 115:     if not 0 <= digit <= system.max_digit:
 116:         raise ValueError(f"digit {digit} is outside {system.name}'s alphabet")
 117:     d = system.dimension
 118:     rows = [[0] * (d + 1) for _ in range(d + 1)]
 119:     rows[0][0] = 1
 120:     rows[0][1] = digit
 121:     for index in range(d - 1):
 122:         rows[index + 1][index + 2] = 1
 123:     for index, coefficient in enumerate(system.recurrence):
 124:         rows[d][index + 1] = coefficient
 125:     return tuple(tuple(row) for row in rows)
 126: 
 127: 
 128: def block_matrix(system: LinearSystem, word: Sequence[int]) -> Matrix:
 129:     matrix = identity(system.dimension + 1)
 130:     for digit in word:
 131:         matrix = matmul(digit_matrix(system, digit), matrix)
 132:     return matrix
 133: 
 134: 
 135: def weights(system: LinearSystem, count: int) -> list[int]:
 136:     values = list(system.initials)
 137:     while len(values) < count:
 138:         values.append(
 139:             sum(
 140:                 coefficient * values[-system.dimension + index]
 141:                 for index, coefficient in enumerate(system.recurrence)
 142:             )
 143:         )
 144:     return values[:count]
 145: 
 146: 
 147: def value(system: LinearSystem, word: Sequence[int]) -> int:
 148:     return sum(digit * weight for digit, weight in zip(word, weights(system, len(word))))
 149: 
 150: 
 151: def greedy_word(system: LinearSystem, number: int) -> Word:
 152:     if number <= 0:
 153:         return ()
 154:     available = list(system.initials)
 155:     while available[-1] <= number:
 156:         available.append(
 157:             sum(
 158:                 coefficient * available[-system.dimension + index]
 159:                 for index, coefficient in enumerate(system.recurrence)
 160:             )
 161:         )
 162:     if available[-1] > number:
 163:         available.pop()
 164:     digits_msd = []
 165:     remainder = number
 166:     for weight in reversed(available):
 167:         digit, remainder = divmod(remainder, weight)
 168:         if digit > system.max_digit:
 169:             raise ValueError(f"digit bound too small for {system.name}")
 170:         digits_msd.append(digit)
 171:     if remainder:
 172:         raise ValueError(f"greedy expansion failed for {system.name}")
 173:     return tuple(reversed(digits_msd))
 174: 
 175: 
 176: def is_canonical(system: LinearSystem, word: Sequence[int]) -> bool:
 177:     if not word or word[-1] == 0:
 178:         return False
 179:     if system.name == "fibonacci":
 180:         return all(left + right <= 1 for left, right in zip(word, word[1:]))
 181:     return tuple(word) == greedy_word(system, value(system, word))
 182: 
 183: 
 184: def matrix_order(matrix: Matrix, modulus: int, limit: int = 2_000_000) -> int:
 185:     if modulus < 2:
 186:         raise ValueError("modulus must be at least 2")
 187:     if gcd(determinant(matrix), modulus) != 1:
 188:         raise NonInvertibleAction(
 189:             f"determinant {determinant(matrix)} is not a unit modulo {modulus}"
 190:         )
 191:     reduced = tuple(tuple(entry % modulus for entry in row) for row in matrix)
 192:     target = identity(len(matrix))
 193:     power = target
 194:     for exponent in range(1, limit + 1):
 195:         power = matmul(reduced, power, modulus)
 196:         if power == target:
 197:             return exponent
 198:     raise RuntimeError(f"matrix order exceeded search limit {limit}")

 201: def pumped_word(word: Word, cuts: tuple[int, int, int, int], exponent: int) -> Word:
 202:     a, b, c, d = cuts
 203:     if not 0 <= a <= b <= c <= d <= len(word):
 204:         raise ValueError("cuts must be ordered positions in the word")
 205:     u, v, x, y, z = word[:a], word[a:b], word[b:c], word[c:d], word[d:]
 206:     if not v and not y:
 207:         raise ValueError("at least one pumped block must be nonempty")
 208:     return u + v * exponent + x + y * exponent + z
 209: 
 210: 
 211: def pump_disjoint_blocks(
 212:     word: Word, spans: Sequence[tuple[int, int]], exponent: int
 213: ) -> Word:
 214:     if exponent < 0:
 215:         raise ValueError("the pumping exponent must be nonnegative")
 216:     previous = 0
 217:     pieces: list[int] = []
 218:     nonempty = False
 219:     for start, stop in spans:
 220:         if not previous <= start <= stop <= len(word):
 221:             raise ValueError("pumped spans must be ordered and disjoint")
 222:         pieces.extend(word[previous:start])
 223:         block = word[start:stop]
 224:         pieces.extend(block * exponent)
 225:         nonempty = nonempty or bool(block)
 226:         previous = stop
 227:     if not nonempty:
 228:         raise ValueError("at least one pumped block must be nonempty")
 229:     pieces.extend(word[previous:])
 230:     return tuple(pieces)

 233: def tail_action_state(
 234:     recurrence: Sequence[int],
 235:     weight_values: Sequence[int],
 236:     prefix: Word,
 237:     suffix: Word,
 238: ) -> tuple[int, ...]:
 239:     """Apply suffix matrices to the state seeded after a fixed prefix."""
 240:     dimension = len(recurrence)
 241:     start = len(prefix)
 242:     required = start + len(suffix) + dimension
 243:     if dimension < 1 or len(weight_values) < required:
 244:         raise ValueError("insufficient recurrence or weight data for the suffix")
 245:     for index in range(start, len(weight_values) - dimension):
 246:         expected = sum(
 247:             recurrence[offset] * weight_values[index + offset]
 248:             for offset in range(dimension)
 249:         )
 250:         if weight_values[index + dimension] != expected:
 251:             raise ValueError("the supplied recurrence is not valid after the prefix")
 252: 
 253:     max_digit = max(prefix + suffix, default=0)
 254:     tail_system = LinearSystem(
 255:         name="tail_action",
 256:         polynomial="",
 257:         recurrence=tuple(recurrence),
 258:         initials=tuple(weight_values[start : start + dimension]),
 259:         max_digit=max_digit,
 260:     )
 261:     prefix_value = sum(
 262:         digit * weight for digit, weight in zip(prefix, weight_values)
 263:     )
 264:     seed = (prefix_value,) + tuple(weight_values[start : start + dimension])
 265:     return matvec(block_matrix(tail_system, suffix), seed)

 268: def linear_mcfg_ray_word(
 269:     prefix: Word,
 270:     constants: Sequence[Word],
 271:     left_pumps: Sequence[Word],
 272:     middles: Sequence[Word],
 273:     right_pumps: Sequence[Word],
 274:     exponent: int,
 275: ) -> Word:
 276:     """Evaluate the word produced after ``exponent`` ray recursions."""
 277:     if exponent < 0:
 278:         raise ValueError("the ray exponent must be nonnegative")
 279:     fan_out = len(left_pumps)
 280:     if not (
 281:         len(constants) == fan_out + 1
 282:         and len(middles) == fan_out
 283:         and len(right_pumps) == fan_out
 284:     ):
 285:         raise ValueError("inconsistent synchronized-ray block counts")
 286:     if not any(left_pumps) and not any(right_pumps):
 287:         raise ValueError("at least one synchronized pump must be nonempty")
 288: 
 289:     word = list(prefix)
 290:     for index in range(fan_out):
 291:         word.extend(constants[index])
 292:         word.extend(left_pumps[index] * exponent)
 293:         word.extend(middles[index])
 294:         word.extend(right_pumps[index] * exponent)
 295:     word.extend(constants[-1])
 296:     return tuple(word)

 640:             for _ in range(maximum_index + 1):
 641:                 following = tuple(entry % modulus for entry in matvec(companion, state))
 642:                 twice = tuple(entry % modulus for entry in matvec(companion, following))
 643:                 check(
 644:                     all(
 645:                         (twice[index] - following[index] - state[index]) % modulus
 646:                         == 0
 647:                         for index in range(3)
 648:                     )
 649:                 )
 650:                 check(any(entry % modulus for entry in state))
 651:                 state = following
 652: 
 653:     return {
 654:         "cases": cases,
 655:         "failures": failures,
 656:         "reachable_rank": 2,
 657:         "reachable_determinant": reachable_determinant,
 658:     }
 659:
